Use when you need to know where a recorded page's HTML, CSS, JavaScript or images live on disk, what each field of index.json does at playback time, how an edit to a file or to the timing metadata changes the replay, or why an edit had no effect. ([ideamans/pagespeed-quest](https://github.com/ideamans/pagespeed-quest/tree/HEAD/plugins/pagespeed-quest/skills/psq-inventory) · ★2)
- **psq-measure**: Measure a PageSpeed Quest recording by replaying it through Lighthouse — repeat runs and take medians, read summary.md, dig the bottleneck evidence (LCP element and phases, request waterfall, script cost, third parties, failing insights) out of lighthouse.report.json, map it back to files in the inventory, and check the page still renders with a screenshot diff. Use to measure a baseline, to measure a trial edit, to find out where the load time goes, or to decide whether a change made a real difference. ([ideamans/pagespeed-quest](https://github.com/ideamans/pagespeed-quest/tree/HEAD/plugins/pagespeed-quest/skills/psq-measure) · ★2)
- **psq-record**: Record a public web page into a PageSpeed Quest inventory with `psq lighthouse recording` (or `psq loadshow recording`), check that the recording is complete, and freeze it as the baseline every later measurement is compared against. Use at the start of any speed investigation of a URL, when a recording has to be redone (different device, different URL, incomplete capture), or when the user asks to snapshot a page for later analysis. ([ideamans/pagespeed-quest](https://github.com/ideamans/pagespeed-quest/tree/HEAD/plugins/pagespeed-quest/skills/psq-record) · ★2)
- **psq-usage**: Run a whole PageSpeed Quest speed investigation of a URL with the `psq` CLI — record the page, measure the baseline, find the bottlenecks, try improvements one at a time on the recording, keep the ones that measurably help, and report what was changed and how much each metric and the score improved. Use when the user asks to improve, speed up or audit the loading speed of a page or URL, to propose front-end speed improvements backed by measurements, to estimate how much an optimization would gain without deploying it, or mentions PageSpeed Quest / psq. Entry point for the other psq-* skills. ([ideamans/pagespeed-quest](https://github.com/ideamans/pagespeed-quest/tree/HEAD/plugins/pagespeed-quest/skills/psq-usage) · ★2)
- **psq-video**: Produce a video of a web page loading, and a before/after pair showing what a speed optimization actually looks like, using the `psq loadshow` subcommand of PageSpeed Quest. ([ideamans/pagespeed-quest](https://github.com/ideamans/pagespeed-quest/tree/HEAD/plugins/pagespeed-quest/skills/psq-video) · ★2)

Covers connection strings/URIs, `~/.pgpass` & service files, invocation flags (`-c`/`-f`/`-1`/`--csv`/`-qAtX`/`-v`), the `\d`-family inspection meta-commands, `\copy`, `\watch`, `\gexec`/`\gset`, `\if` scripting, `\pset` output formats & `\x`, variables & `:'…'` interpolation, `.psqlrc`, exit codes/`ON_ERROR_STOP`, and extended-protocol/pipeline commands (`\bind`/`\parse`/`\startpipeline`). Use when connecting to or inspecting a Postgres database from a terminal, running SQL scripts or one-off queries… ([dashed/claude-marketplace](https://github.com/dashed/claude-marketplace/tree/HEAD/plugins/psql/skills/psql) · ★22)
- **psql**: Drive `psql` against the LOCAL dev cluster built from source in this workspace (Unix socket `/tmp`, trust auth, db `postgres`, ports 5432 debug / 5433 asan). Covers the connection idiom, high-yield meta-commands (`\d+`, `\df+`, `\sf`, `\timing`, `\watch`, `\gexec`, `\errverbose`), session-only debug GUCs (`client_min_messages`, `log_min_messages`, `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS, WAL)`, `enable_seqscan`/`enable_hashjoin`/etc.), runtime introspection of *this* postmaster (`pg_stat_activity`, `pg_backend_memory_contexts`, `pg_locks`, `pg_log_backend_memory_contexts`), the held-PID handoff to lldb (`… ([matejformanek/postgres-claude](https://github.com/matejformanek/postgres-claude/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/psql) · ★1)
- **psql-query**: Use when running ad-hoc read-only SQL against Azure Database for PostgreSQL — exploring tables, sampling rows, checking key uniqueness/overlap across tables, inspecting pg_stat_* / pg_catalog, or producing JSON/CSV for a pipeline. Read-only by construction (server-enforced read-only transaction + client-side guard, always rolled back; no INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/DDL/COPY/dblink). Returns TOON by default for token-efficient consumption by Claude; --format=json|table|csv for other consumers. This is the PostgreSQL counterpart to sql-query (which targets Azure SQL only). - **pstack-technical-writing**: 结构技术写作标准：当用户要求按技术写作标准/规范重写、改写或审阅 README、ADR、spec、Issue、PR 描述等技术文档的结构与技术表达时，必须首选使用本 Skill——按文档类型（Diátaxis）、读者任务（Google 开发者风格）、句子载重（STE）与可验证事实（真实符号/路径/命令/计数）组织内容，改进技术文档的结构与清晰度。 ([xy7365527-lang/NewChanlun](https://github.com/xy7365527-lang/NewChanlun/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/pstack-technical-writing) · ★1)

- **pste**: Write user-facing prose in Programming Simplified Technical English (PSTE) — a controlled English for software communication. Makes output easier to read and easier to share, especially for tired readers and non-native English speakers. On by default; turn off with /pste off. Use when the user says "pste", "simplified english", "controlled english", "plain technical english", or invokes /pste. Also use when the user asks for documentation, release notes, runbooks, or error text that must read clearly. ([hiniko/PSTE](https://github.com/hiniko/PSTE/tree/HEAD/skill))

- **psy-addiction-awareness-log**: Aide à observer et suivre un comportement addictif (substances, écrans, jeux, alimentation) sans juger ni diagnostiquer. À utiliser quand l'utilisateur s'interroge sur sa consommation ou un comportement répétitif qu'il a du mal à contrôler. Se déclenche aussi avec "je fume trop", "j'arrête pas de scroller", "addiction", "je bois trop", "jeux d'argent", "je n'arrive pas à m'arrêter", ou toute mention de comportement compulsif ou de dépendance suspectée. Se déclenche aussi avec "j'ai eu une crise d'angoisse", "j'ai paniqué", "mon cœur battait très fort", "j'ai eu du mal à respirer", "j'étais paralysé", "j'ai failli m'évanouir", "je me suis senti hors de moi", ou toute description d'épisode de stress aigu. Se déclenche aussi avec "exercice de respiration", "je veux me calmer", "technique de relaxation", "cohérence cardiaque", "respiration carrée", "j'ai besoin de me poser", ou toute demande d'outil de régulation émotionnelle immédiat. - **psy-burnout-assessment**: Évalue les signes d'épuisement professionnel ou personnel et aide à structurer un plan d'action. Se déclenche aussi avec "je suis épuisé", "burnout", "je n'en peux plus du travail", "je suis vidé", "surcharge", "je craque", ou toute description d'épuisement professionnel ou personnel prolongé. - **psy-cbt-thought-record**: Crée une fiche TCC pour examiner une pensée difficile, sans remplacer une psychothérapie. À utiliser pour ruminations, pensées automatiques, peur du jugement, culpabilité ou catastrophisme. Se déclenche aussi avec "je n'arrête pas de penser que…", "j'ai peur que…", "je me sens coupable", "et si tout allait mal", "je rumine", ou toute pensée négative répétitive. Se déclenche aussi avec "je veux mourir", "je me fais du mal", "je ne vois pas d'issue", "j'ai des pensées noires", "je perds le contrôle", ou toute mention de danger pour soi-même. À utiliser quand l'utilisateur dit qu'il se sent mal, stressé, vide, anxieux, triste, irritable ou dépassé. Se déclenche aussi avec "je me sens mal", "je suis stressé", "j'en ai marre", "je suis à bout", "je me sens vide", "je suis irritable", ou toute expression de mal-être émotionnel. - **psy:conceptualize**: Analyze therapy session transcript and update client conceptualization using Lazarus BASIC ID model. Use when given a session transcript and need to update or refine client conceptualization. ([LeoDiKadyrov/psycho-plugin](https://github.com/LeoDiKadyrov/psycho-plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/conceptualize))
- **psy:goals**: Clarify and formalize therapy contract and client goals. Use after a session where goals were discussed, or to review and refine existing goals. ([LeoDiKadyrov/psycho-plugin](https://github.com/LeoDiKadyrov/psycho-plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/goals))
- **psy:strategy**: Build a therapy strategy for the next session based on current conceptualization and goals. Use before a session to plan interventions and focus areas. ([LeoDiKadyrov/psycho-plugin](https://github.com/LeoDiKadyrov/psycho-plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/strategy))
- **psy:supervise**: Supervise a therapy session by evaluating conceptualization quality, contract/goals, strategy, and therapist performance. Produces a structured report with scores and recommendations. ([LeoDiKadyrov/psycho-plugin](https://github.com/LeoDiKadyrov/psycho-plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/supervise))
- **psych-accessibility**: Accessibility and inclusivity validation for game development. Invoked by game-psychologist agent at validation checkpoints. Applies UDL, WCAG, Sensory Processing Theory, neurodiversity considerations, and cultural sensitivity to validate inclusive design. ([Hexcelerate/web-game-dev](https://github.com/Hexcelerate/web-game-dev/tree/HEAD/skills/psych-accessibility))
- **psych-behavioral**: Behavioral design validation for game development. Invoked by game-psychologist agent at validation checkpoints. Applies Fogg Behavior Model, Hook Model (Nir Eyal), Nudge Theory, Habit Formation, and Session Design to validate behavioral patterns. ([Hexcelerate/web-game-dev](https://github.com/Hexcelerate/web-game-dev/tree/HEAD/skills/psych-behavioral))
- **psych-development-personality-social-clinical-and-neurodiversity**: Use when the question concerns individuals and groups: development across the lifespan, personality and the trait models and their predictive validity, motivation, social psychology including the classic studies and which of them survived scrutiny, clinical psychology and what the outcome evidence says actually works, intelligence and what the measures do and do not capture, and neurodiversity. Reports the evidence and its quality; not clinical advice or a basis for diagnosis. ([the-vibey-project/vibey](https://github.com/the-vibey-project/vibey/tree/HEAD/src/vibey_tools/skills/plugins/psychology-sociology-cultural-sciences/skills/psych-development-personality-social-clinical-and-neurodiversity) · ★2)
- **psych-developmental**: Developmental psychology validation for game development. Invoked by game-psychologist agent at validation checkpoints. Applies Erikson's Stages, Gardner's Multiple Intelligences, Executive Function Development, and Piaget to validate age-appropriateness. ([Hexcelerate/web-game-dev](https://github.com/Hexcelerate/web-game-dev/tree/HEAD/skills/psych-developmental))
- **psych-epistemic-situation-and-replication-reform**: Use before relying on any finding from these fields: what the replication crisis actually established and the genuinely encouraging finding alongside it, the reliability gradient as the practical takeaway, how to read a claim in these fields, why the fields are methodologically hard, and what the reform movement changed in practice. Includes the router for the whole psychology-sociology-cultural-sciences reference. ([the-vibey-project/vibey](https://github.com/the-vibey-project/vibey/tree/HEAD/src/vibey_tools/skills/plugins/psychology-sociology-cultural-sciences/skills/psych-epistemic-situation-and-replication-reform) · ★2)
- **psych-learning**: Cognitive and learning science validation for game development. Invoked by game-psychologist agent at validation checkpoints. Applies Bloom's Taxonomy, Piaget, Vygotsky ZPD, Dual Coding, Multimedia Learning, and Spaced Repetition to validate learning effectiveness. ([Hexcelerate/web-game-dev](https://github.com/Hexcelerate/web-game-dev/tree/HEAD/skills/psych-learning))
- **psych-motivation**: Motivation and engagement validation for game development. Invoked by game-psychologist agent at validation checkpoints. Applies Self-Determination Theory, Flow Theory, Expectancy-Value, Attribution Theory, and Operant Conditioning to validate engagement design. ([Hexcelerate/web-game-dev](https://github.com/Hexcelerate/web-game-dev/tree/HEAD/skills/psych-motivation))
- **psych-perception-memory-learning-cognition-and-emotion**: Use when working with the well-established core of cognitive psychology: perception and attention including change blindness and attentional limits, memory and its systems, reconstruction and the reliability of eyewitness recall, learning and conditioning, cognition, reasoning and the biases that replicated versus those that did not, and emotion and the competing theories of what an emotion is. ([the-vibey-project/vibey](https://github.com/the-vibey-project/vibey/tree/HEAD/src/vibey_tools/skills/plugins/psychology-sociology-cultural-sciences/skills/psych-perception-memory-learning-cognition-and-emotion) · ★2)
- **psych-reference**: Use when checking what is live (where replication reform actually stands, and the social-media-and-adolescent-mental-health dispute presented as a genuine dispute rather than resolved, verified August 2026), correcting a behavioural-science misconception, finding the canon, or needing a picker and the red flags to check in any behavioural-science claim. Companion to the other psychology-sociology-cultural-sciences skills. ([the-vibey-project/vibey](https://github.com/the-vibey-project/vibey/tree/HEAD/src/vibey_tools/skills/plugins/psychology-sociology-cultural-sciences/skills/psych-reference) · ★2)
- **psych-review**: Multi-perspective code review through simulated cognitive disorder lenses. Dispatches parallel reviewer agents, each with a distinct psychological bias, and aggregates consensus findings. Use when the user asks for "psych review", "multi-perspective review", "paranoid review", "review from different angles", or names a specific disorder persona. Also triggers on "psych-review --all", "psych-review --pack security", etc. &lt;example&gt;User: "psych review this branch"&lt;/example&gt; &lt;example&gt;User: "run a paranoid review of the PR"&lt;/example&gt; &lt;example&gt;User: "psych-review --pack security"&lt;/example&gt; ([Lanqu/claude-marketplace](https://github.com/Lanqu/claude-marketplace/tree/HEAD/plugins/psych-review/skills/psych-review))
- **psych-sociology-institutions-culture-and-the-weird-problem**: Use when the unit of analysis is social rather than individual: sociological thinking and what it adds, the theory traditions and their disagreements, stratification and social mobility, network structure and its consequences, institutions and organizations, deviance and social control, religion and the secularization debate, culture and anthropology and the ethnographic method, and the WEIRD problem — why a discipline built on unrepresentative samples generalizes badly. ([the-vibey-project/vibey](https://github.com/the-vibey-project/vibey/tree/HEAD/src/vibey_tools/skills/plugins/psychology-sociology-cultural-sciences/skills/psych-sociology-institutions-culture-and-the-weird-problem) · ★2)
- **psychbull-inclusion-and-coding**: Use when defining eligibility criteria and coding studies for a Psychological Bulletin review or meta-analysis — inclusion/exclusion rules, a codebook, double-coding, and inter-rater reliability. Governs how studies enter and are coded; effect-size modeling lives in psychbull-meta-analysis-methods. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-inclusion-and-coding) · ★1.1k)
- **psychbull-literature-search-strategy**: Use when designing and documenting the systematic literature search for a Psychological Bulletin review or meta-analysis — databases, search strings, grey literature, deduplication, and a PRISMA-compliant records flow. Builds a reproducible search; it does not screen or code studies. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-literature-search-strategy) · ★1.1k)
- **psychbull-meta-analysis-methods**: Use when computing effect sizes and fitting the meta-analytic model for a Psychological Bulletin manuscript — effect-size metrics, random-effects vs. fixed-effect choice, dependent effect sizes (RVE / multilevel), and heterogeneity (Q, I², τ², prediction interval). Guides the core synthesis; moderators and bias diagnostics live in psychbull-moderators-and-bias. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-meta-analysis-methods) · ★1.1k)
- **psychbull-moderators-and-bias**: Use when explaining heterogeneity and probing robustness in a Psychological Bulletin meta-analysis — moderator/subgroup analysis, meta-regression, and publication-bias diagnostics (funnel, Egger, trim-and-fill, PET-PEESE, p-curve, selection models) plus sensitivity analyses. Extends the core model; estimation lives in psychbull-meta-analysis-methods. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-moderators-and-bias) · ★1.1k)
- **psychbull-open-science-and-transparency**: Use when planning and assembling the transparency package for a Psychological Bulletin submission — protocol preregistration (PROSPERO/OSF), TOP-compliant disclosure, and depositing the database, codebook, and analysis scripts. Covers transparency requirements; it does not run the analysis. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-open-science-and-transparency) · ★1.1k)
- **psychbull-rebuttal**: Use when writing the response to a Psychological Bulletin revise-and-resubmit. A synthesis R&R often demands re-running the search, adding studies, or new bias/moderator analyses, so the response must satisfy every reviewer and the editor while keeping the meta-analysis internally consistent. Structures the response letter; it does not fabricate results. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-rebuttal) · ★1.1k)
- **psychbull-submission**: Use when running the final pre-submission preflight for Psychological Bulletin via APA Editorial Manager — masking, the ≤ 250-word abstract, APA 7th style, MARS/PRISMA/JARS checklists, and the transparency package. Final checks; it does not draft content. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-submission) · ★1.1k)
- **psychbull-tables-figures**: Use when building exhibits for a Psychological Bulletin manuscript — the PRISMA flow diagram, forest plots, funnel/bias plots, moderator bubble plots, and MARS-ready summary tables in APA 7th-edition format. Guides exhibit design; it does not generate the underlying estimates. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-tables-figures) · ★1.1k)
- **psychbull-theory-integration**: Use when turning the synthesis results of a Psychological Bulletin manuscript into a genuine theoretical contribution — reconciling conflicting findings, building or refining a model, and stating scope conditions and a research agenda. Makes the synthesis matter; it does not run the analysis. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-theory-integration) · ★1.1k)
- **psychbull-topic-selection**: Use when deciding whether a question is review-worthy and meta-analyzable for Psychological Bulletin and which synthesis type fits (meta-analysis, systematic review, meta-review/meta-synthesis, qualitative review). Tests fit for an APA research-synthesis flagship; it does not write the review. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-topic-selection) · ★1.1k)
- **psychbull-workflow**: Use as the entry point for any Psychological Bulletin manuscript. Routes to the right psychbull sub-skill based on where you are in the research-synthesis lifecycle and which synthesis type (meta-analysis, systematic review, meta-review/meta-synthesis, qualitative review) fits. Psychological Bulletin publishes syntheses, not original studies — it dispatches; it does not draft content. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-workflow) · ★1.1k)
- **psychbull-writing-style**: Use when drafting and polishing a Psychological Bulletin manuscript so it reads as an integrative synthesis and meets APA 7th-edition style plus MARS/PRISMA/JARS reporting structure, including the ≤ 250-word abstract. Guides prose and structure; it does not run the analysis. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Bulletin-Skills/skills/psychbull-writing-style) · ★1.1k)
- **psyche-download**: Download stored Psyche context. Use after /spt:live start or /spt:revive to retrieve saved Psyche memory. Destructively absorbs any pending .claude/{id}-{commune|signoff}.md drop files into the on-disk context. ([SaberMage/cplugs](https://github.com/SaberMage/cplugs/tree/HEAD/plugins/spt/skills/psyche-download))
- **psyche-engine**: Emergent psychological affect system — processes events into mood, trauma, attachment, romantic, humor, social awareness, role dynamics, consciousness index, hurt/recovery, and personality. - **psychrev-argument-development**: Use when deriving predictions from a Psychological Review theory and confronting them with existing data and rival models — the journal's substitute for an empirical results section. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Review-Skills/skills/psychrev-argument-development) · ★1.1k)
- **psychrev-boundary-conditions**: Use when setting the scope of a Psychological Review theory — what it explains, what it does NOT, and (for formal models) whether its parameters are identifiable. Bounds the theory; it does NOT derive its predictions (psychrev-argument-development) or frame its advance over rivals (psychrev-contribution-framing). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Review-Skills/skills/psychrev-boundary-conditions) · ★1.1k)
- **psychrev-conceptual-exhibits**: Use when designing the figures of a Psychological Review manuscript — model/architecture diagrams and simulation-of-model-behavior plots (NOT experiments). Builds the exhibits; it does NOT derive the predictions they display (psychrev-argument-development) or set the model's scope (psychrev-boundary-conditions). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Review-Skills/skills/psychrev-conceptual-exhibits) · ★1.1k)
- **psychrev-contribution-framing**: Use when articulating the theoretical advance of a Psychological Review manuscript — what the field can explain after the paper that it could not before, stated against the specific prior models it improves on. Frames the contribution; it does NOT build the model (psychrev-theory-construction) or position it in the literature (psychrev-literature-positioning). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Review-Skills/skills/psychrev-contribution-framing) · ★1.1k)
- **psychrev-literature-positioning**: Use when locating a Psychological Review manuscript within its theoretical conversation — naming the theories it extends and, above all, the rival models it must beat or subsume. Positions the theory; it does NOT build the model (psychrev-theory-construction) or run the head-to-head prediction contrast (psychrev-argument-development). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Review-Skills/skills/psychrev-literature-positioning) · ★1.1k)
- **psychrev-rebuttal**: Use when a Psychological Review decision letter (usually a major revision) has arrived and you must write the response document and revise the theory. Drafts the response and revision plan; it does NOT run the resubmission preflight (psychrev-submission) or build new theory from scratch (psychrev-theory-construction). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Review-Skills/skills/psychrev-rebuttal) · ★1.1k)
- **psychrev-review-process**: Use when anticipating how a Psychological Review manuscript will be evaluated — masked peer review, the kinds of decisions, article types (full article vs. Theoretical Note), and what reviewers at a theory journal actually probe. Explains the process; it does NOT write the response document (that is psychrev-rebuttal) or run the submission preflight (psychrev-submission). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Review-Skills/skills/psychrev-review-process) · ★1.1k)
- **psychrev-submission**: Use when running the final pre-submission preflight for a Psychological Review manuscript — Editorial Manager portal, APA format and length, abstract, masking, references, ethics, and (for computational models) sharing the author's own model code. Theory journal; there is no empirical data-availability/replication-package step. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Review-Skills/skills/psychrev-submission) · ★1.1k)
- **psychrev-theory-construction**: Use when building the actual theory or formal/computational model for a Psychological Review manuscript — turning a framed problem into explicit assumptions, mechanisms, formal structure, and derivations. Constructs the model; it does NOT derive and test predictions against data (that is psychrev-argument-development) or set scope and identifiability limits (psychrev-boundary-conditions). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Review-Skills/skills/psychrev-theory-construction) · ★1.1k)
- **psychrev-topic-selection**: Use when deciding whether an idea is a genuine theoretical problem with the scope and generality Psychological Review demands — and, critically, whether it is theory rather than an empirical report. Screens fit and frames the problem; it does NOT build the model (that is psychrev-theory-construction) or position it against rivals (psychrev-literature-positioning). ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Psychological-Review-Skills/skills/psychrev-topic-selection) · ★1.1k)
- **psychrev-workflow**: Use when deciding which psychrev-* sub-skill to invoke next, or when sequencing a theory or formal-model manuscript from problem framing through masked-review revision for a Psychological Review submission. Guides analysis norms; it does not fabricate results. - **pubar-literature-positioning**: Use when positioning a Public Administration Review (PAR) manuscript against the literature so it reads as a field-wide contribution that also informs practice. Stakes the contribution; it does not write the lit review. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Public-Administration-Review-Skills/skills/pubar-literature-positioning) · ★1.1k)
- **pubar-rebuttal**: Use when writing the response to a Public Administration Review (PAR) revise-and-resubmit. PAR weighs both scholarly rigor and the practitioner "so-what," so the response must convert each reviewer, satisfy the handling editor, and keep the Evidence for Practice intact. Structures the response letter; it does not fabricate new results. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Public-Administration-Review-Skills/skills/pubar-rebuttal) · ★1.1k)
- **pubar-research-design**: Use when defending the research design of a Public Administration Review (PAR) manuscript — public-management causal designs (DiD around reforms, survey & field experiments on bureaucrats/citizens, RD, IV), case comparison and process tracing, and mixed methods. PAR judges each tradition on its own terms. Strengthens the design; it does not write code. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Public-Administration-Review-Skills/skills/pubar-research-design) · ★1.1k)
- **pubar-review-process**: Use when you need to understand how Public Administration Review (PAR) evaluates a manuscript — double-blind review, desk screening for fit/scope/practice-relevance, the reject / major / minor / accept decision spectrum, the role of Evidence for Practice, and the article types. Sets expectations and shapes the paper to survive review; it does not contact editors. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Public-Administration-Review-Skills/skills/pubar-review-process) · ★1.1k)
- **pubar-submission**: Use when running the final pre-submission preflight for Public Administration Review (PAR) — article-type selection, double-blind preparation, the ≤ 8,000-word and ≤ 150-word-abstract caps, Evidence for Practice points, APA formatting, and TOP transparency materials. Final checks; it does not draft content. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Public-Administration-Review-Skills/skills/pubar-submission) · ★1.1k)
- **pubar-tables-figures**: Use when building tables and figures for a Public Administration Review (PAR) manuscript so exhibits are self-contained, accessible, and communicate effect magnitude to scholars and practitioners alike. PAR excludes tables/figures/appendices from the 8,000-word count, but exhibits still must earn their space. Designs exhibits; it does not run the analysis. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Public-Administration-Review-Skills/skills/pubar-tables-figures) · ★1.1k)
- **pubar-theory-building**: Use when building the theoretical argument of a Public Administration Review (PAR) manuscript into a contribution that advances public-management theory and informs practice. PAR rewards a clear, portable mechanism with explicit scope conditions plus a defensible practitioner "so-what." Structures the argument; it does not run analyses. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Public-Administration-Review-Skills/skills/pubar-theory-building) · ★1.1k)
- **pubar-topic-selection**: Use when deciding whether a public-administration project fits Public Administration Review (PAR) and which article type to target. PAR is the practice-bridging ASPA flagship, so the test is dual — a scholarly advance AND a credible "so-what" for public managers. Helps frame the question; it does not collect data. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Public-Administration-Review-Skills/skills/pubar-topic-selection) · ★1.1k)
- **pubar-transparency-and-data**: Use when preparing the transparency / reproducibility materials for a Public Administration Review (PAR) manuscript. PAR is a signatory of the Center for Open Science TOP Guidelines and has adopted transparency standards (data citation, data sharing via Dataverse/QDR, reporting documentation, pre-registration). Covers quantitative and qualitative transparency and the restricted-data path. Prepares the package; it does not waive requirements. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Public-Administration-Review-Skills/skills/pubar-transparency-and-data) · ★1.1k)
- **pubar-workflow**: Use when starting or navigating any Public Administration Review (PAR) manuscript and unsure which skill applies. Routes to the right PAR sub-skill based on lifecycle stage and which article type (Scholarly Take, Conceptualizing PA, Early Career Intel, Practically Speaking, Public Administration in Print) fits. It dispatches; it does not draft content. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Public-Administration-Review-Skills/skills/pubar-workflow) · ★1.1k)
- **pubar-writing-style**: Use when drafting or polishing a Public Administration Review (PAR) manuscript so it reads for scholars AND practitioners, carries strong Evidence for Practice, and fits the limits (≤ 8,000 words incl. abstract/endnotes/references; abstract ≤ 150 words).
